+/// Which tokens are a macro's input: inside `name!(…)`, `name![…]`,
+/// `name!{…}`, or the body of `macro_rules! name {…}`.
+fn macro_inputs(tokens: &[Tok]) -> Vec<bool> {
+    let mut inside = vec![false; tokens.len()];
+    for at in 0..tokens.len() {
+        if !matches!(tokens[at], Tok::Ident(..)) || tokens.get(at + 1) != Some(&Tok::Punct('!')) {
+            continue;
+        }
+        let mut open = at + 2;
+        if keyword(tokens.get(at), "macro_rules")
+            && matches!(tokens.get(open), Some(Tok::Ident(..)))
+        {
+            open += 1;
+        }
+        let close = match tokens.get(open) {
+            Some(Tok::Punct('(')) => Tok::Punct(')'),
+            Some(Tok::Punct('[')) => Tok::Punct(']'),
+            Some(Tok::Open) => Tok::Close,
+            _ => continue,
+        };
+        let opening = &tokens[open];
+        let mut depth = 0;
+        for index in open..tokens.len() {
+            if tokens[index] == *opening {
+                depth += 1;
+            } else if tokens[index] == close {
+                depth -= 1;
+                if depth == 0 {
+                    break;
+                }
+            }
+            inside[index] = true;
+        }
+    }
+    inside
+}
+

+/// The crate roots: the library, each binary the manifest names, and Cargo's
+/// default binary paths. A `mod name;` in a root loads from the root's own
+/// directory, as in a `mod.rs`.
+fn crate_roots(manifest: &Toml) -> BTreeSet<PathBuf> {
+    let path = |target: &Toml| target.get("path").and_then(Toml::str).map(clean);
+    let mut roots: BTreeSet<PathBuf> = ["src/lib.rs", "src/main.rs"]
+        .into_iter()
+        .map(PathBuf::from)
+        .collect();

+    roots.extend(
+        manifest
+            .get("bin")
+            .map_or(&[][..], Toml::items)
+            .iter()
+            .filter_map(path),
+    );
+    roots
+}

-- implementation 1 --
     1	//! AC-1's static half: the room reaches no generator, verifier or LLM code.
     2	//!
     3	//! Generation, verification and every model call live in the offline pipeline
     4	//! (`pipeline/`, the Python package `popquiz`). The room must have no way to
     5	//! call into any of it. This file reads the room's manifest, its lock and its
     6	//! source, and refuses:
     7	//!
     8	//! - a dependency in any table (ordinary, optional, feature-gated, dev, build,
     9	//!   per-target, patch, replace) whose crate name is denied, or whose `path`
    10	//!   points into `pipeline/`;
    11	//! - a denied crate anywhere in the lock's subgraph under `room`;
    12	//! - a `use` or `extern crate` in `src/` whose root is a denied name.
    13	//!
    14	//! **The denylist names crates, never folders.** The room's own folder is
    15	//! `popquiz/`, the same word as the Python package, so a check on paths would
    16	//! fail on main or need a carve-out. A dependency carries a name, and names are
    17	//! what is checked; the only path check resolves a `path` dependency against
    18	//! the repository's `pipeline/` folder.
    19	//!
    20	//! The manifest and the lock are read by a small TOML parser and the source by
    21	//! a small Rust tokenizer, both below, so layout (dotted or quoted keys, escaped
    22	//! strings, comments, imports split across lines) does not change what is seen.
    23	//! No crate is added for either.
    24	//!
    25	//! A scan is a tripwire, not a proof: someone who means to can dodge it. Imports
    26	//! a macro writes, and paths used with no `use` at all (`openai::Client::new()`),
    27	//! are left to the manifest and lock checks: a crate the source can name must be
    28	//! declared there first. This is the static half of AC-1 only.
    29	
    30	use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};
    31	use std::fs;
    32	use std::path::{Component, Path, PathBuf};
    33	
    34	// --- The denylist -------------------------------------------------------------
    35	
    36	/// Words that deny a crate when one of them is a whole `_`-separated part of
    37	/// its name: `async-openai` and `cargo-miri` are denied, `generic-array` is not.
    38	/// `popquiz` is the pipeline's Python package (its import and distribution name
    39	/// in `pipeline/pyproject.toml`); `pipeline` is the folder that holds it.
    40	const DENIED_WORDS: &[&str] = &[
    41	    "anthropic",
    42	    "openai",
    43	    "llm",
    44	    "generator",
    45	    "miri",
    46	    "pipeline",
    47	    "popquiz",
    48	];
    49	
    50	/// The compiler's own crates (`rustc_driver`, `rustc_interface`, …), which is
    51	/// how Miri or an in-process verifier would reach rustc. This also refuses
    52	/// crates.io's `rustc-hash` and `rustc_version`. Neither is in the lock today;
    53	/// adding one is a person's call, not a reason to loosen this.
    54	const DENIED_PREFIX: &str = "rustc_";
    55	
    56	/// Whether a crate name is denied. Cargo treats `-` and `_` in a name as the
    57	/// same character, so this does too.
    58	fn denied(name: &str) -> bool {
    59	    let name = name.trim().to_ascii_lowercase().replace('-', "_");
    60	    name.starts_with(DENIED_PREFIX) || name.split('_').any(|word| DENIED_WORDS.contains(&word))
    61	}
    62	
    63	const WHY: &str = "AC-1: the room must not reach generator, verifier or LLM code \
    64	                   (popquiz/tests/dependencies.rs)";
    65	
    66	// --- Files --------------------------------------------------------------------
    67	
    68	/// The room crate's folder. Read at run time, so the built test can be pointed
    69	/// at a throwaway copy holding a planted dependency that `cargo --locked`
    70	/// would refuse to build. `cargo test` sets it; the compile-time value is the
    71	/// fallback.
    72	fn crate_dir() -> PathBuf {
    73	    std::env::var_os("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")
    74	        .map(PathBuf::from)
    75	        .unwrap_or_else(|| PathBuf::from(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")))
    76	}
    77	
    78	fn read(path: &Path) -> String {
    79	    fs::read_to_string(path).unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("read {}: {e}", path.display()))
    80	}
    81	
    82	fn rust_files(dir: &Path, out: &mut Vec<PathBuf>) {
    83	    let mut entries: Vec<PathBuf> = fs::read_dir(dir)
    84	        .unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("read {}: {e}", dir.display()))
    85	        .map(|entry| entry.expect("directory entry").path())
    86	        .collect();
    87	    entries.sort();
    88	    for path in entries {
    89	        if path.is_dir() {
    90	            rust_files(&path, out);
    91	        } else if path.extension().is_some_and(|ext| ext == "rs") {
    92	            out.push(path);
    93	        }
    94	    }
    95	}
    96	
    97	/// `a/b/../c` as `a/c`, without touching the filesystem.
    98	fn normalize(path: &Path) -> PathBuf {
    99	    let mut out = PathBuf::new();
   100	    for component in path.components() {
   101	        match component {
   102	            Component::ParentDir => {
   103	                out.pop();
   104	            }
   105	            Component::CurDir => {}
   106	            other => out.push(other.as_os_str()),
   107	        }
   108	    }
   109	    out
   110	}
   111	
   112	// --- TOML -----------------------------------------------------------------------
   113	//
   114	// Enough of TOML 1.0 for Cargo.toml and Cargo.lock: bare, quoted and dotted
   115	// keys; `[table]` and `[[array]]` headers; the four string kinds with their
   116	// escapes; arrays and inline tables. Numbers, booleans and dates are kept as
   117	// `Other`, since nothing here reads them. Malformed input panics with a line.
   118	
   119	#[derive(Debug)]
   120	enum Toml {
   121	    Str(String),
   122	    Table(Vec<(String, Toml)>),
   123	    Array(Vec<Toml>),
   124	    Other,
   125	}
   126	
   127	impl Toml {
   128	    fn get(&self, key: &str) -> Option<&Toml> {
   129	        self.entries()
   130	            .iter()
   131	            .find(|(name, _)| name == key)
   132	            .map(|(_, value)| value)
   133	    }
   134	
   135	    fn str(&self) -> Option<&str> {
   136	        match self {
   137	            Toml::Str(text) => Some(text),
   138	            _ => None,
   139	        }
   140	    }
   141	
   142	    fn entries(&self) -> &[(String, Toml)] {
   143	        match self {
   144	            Toml::Table(entries) => entries,
   145	            _ => &[],
   146	        }
   147	    }
   148	
   149	    fn items(&self) -> &[Toml] {
   150	        match self {
   151	            Toml::Array(items) => items,
   152	            _ => &[],
   153	        }
   154	    }
   155	}
   156	
   157	/// The table a key path names, created where missing. A path through an array
   158	/// of tables goes into its last element, as a TOML header does.
   159	fn table_at<'a>(
   160	    table: &'a mut Vec<(String, Toml)>,
   161	    path: &[String],
   162	) -> &'a mut Vec<(String, Toml)> {
   163	    let Some((first, rest)) = path.split_first() else {
   164	        return table;
   165	    };
   166	    let index = match table.iter().position(|(name, _)| name == first) {
   167	        Some(index) => index,
   168	        None => {
   169	            table.push((first.clone(), Toml::Table(Vec::new())));
   170	            table.len() - 1
   171	        }
   172	    };
   173	    let child = match &mut table[index].1 {
   174	        Toml::Table(entries) => entries,
   175	        Toml::Array(items) => match items.last_mut() {
   176	            Some(Toml::Table(entries)) => entries,
   177	            _ => panic!("TOML key `{first}` is an array, not an array of tables"),
   178	        },
   179	        _ => panic!("TOML key `{first}` is a value, not a table"),
   180	    };
   181	    table_at(child, rest)
   182	}
   183	
   184	fn insert(table: &mut Vec<(String, Toml)>, key: &[String], value: Toml) {
   185	    let (last, parents) = key.split_last().expect("a key has at least one part");
   186	    table_at(table, parents).push((last.clone(), value));
   187	}
   188	
   189	struct TomlParser {
   190	    text: Vec<char>,
   191	    at: usize,
   192	}
   193	
   194	impl TomlParser {
   195	    fn peek(&self) -> Option<char> {
   196	        self.text.get(self.at).copied()
   197	    }
   198	
   199	    fn looking_at(&self, word: &str) -> bool {
   200	        word.chars()
   201	            .enumerate()
   202	            .all(|(k, c)| self.text.get(self.at + k) == Some(&c))
   203	    }
   204	
   205	    fn fail(&self, what: &str) -> ! {
   206	        let end = self.at.min(self.text.len());
   207	        let line = self.text[..end].iter().filter(|c| **c == '\n').count() + 1;
   208	        panic!("TOML line {line}: {what}")
   209	    }
   210	
   211	    fn expect(&mut self, c: char) {
   212	        if self.peek() != Some(c) {
   213	            self.fail(&format!("expected `{c}`"));
   214	        }
   215	        self.at += 1;
   216	    }
   217	
   218	    fn spaces(&mut self) {
   219	        while matches!(self.peek(), Some(' ' | '\t')) {
   220	            self.at += 1;
   221	        }
   222	    }
   223	
   224	    /// Spaces, line breaks and comments.
   225	    fn blank(&mut self) {
   226	        loop {
   227	            self.spaces();
   228	            match self.peek() {
   229	                Some('\n' | '\r') => self.at += 1,
   230	                Some('#') => {
   231	                    while !matches!(self.peek(), None | Some('\n')) {
   232	                        self.at += 1;
   233	                    }
   234	                }
   235	                _ => return,
   236	            }
   237	        }
   238	    }
   239	
   240	    fn key(&mut self) -> Vec<String> {
   241	        let mut parts = Vec::new();
   242	        loop {
   243	            self.spaces();
   244	            let part = match self.peek() {
   245	                Some('"') => self.basic_string(),
   246	                Some('\'') => self.literal_string(),
   247	                _ => {
   248	                    let start = self.at;
   249	                    while self
   250	                        .peek()
   251	                        .is_some_and(|c| c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || c == '_' || c == '-')
   252	                    {
   253	                        self.at += 1;
   254	                    }
   255	                    if start == self.at {
   256	                        self.fail("expected a key");
   257	                    }
   258	                    self.text[start..self.at].iter().collect()
   259	                }
   260	            };
   261	            parts.push(part);
   262	            self.spaces();
   263	            if self.peek() != Some('.') {
   264	                return parts;
   265	            }
   266	            self.at += 1;
   267	        }
   268	    }
   269	
   270	    /// The end of a multi-line string: three quotes, and up to two more that
   271	    /// belong to the content.
   272	    fn closes_multiline(&mut self, quote: char, out: &mut String) -> bool {
   273	        let mut run = 0;
   274	        while self.text.get(self.at + run) == Some(&quote) {
   275	            run += 1;
   276	        }
   277	        if run < 3 {
   278	            return false;
   279	        }
   280	        let extra = (run - 3).min(2);
   281	        out.extend(std::iter::repeat_n(quote, extra));
   282	        self.at += 3 + extra;
   283	        true
   284	    }
   285	
   286	    fn skip_first_newline(&mut self) {
   287	        if self.looking_at("\r\n") {
   288	            self.at += 2;
   289	        } else if self.looking_at("\n") {
   290	            self.at += 1;
   291	        }
   292	    }
   293	
   294	    fn basic_string(&mut self) -> String {
   295	        let multi = self.looking_at("\"\"\"");
   296	        self.at += if multi { 3 } else { 1 };
   297	        if multi {
   298	            self.skip_first_newline();
   299	        }
   300	        let mut out = String::new();
   301	        loop {
   302	            let Some(c) = self.peek() else {
   303	                self.fail("unterminated string");
   304	            };
   305	            if multi && c == '"' && self.closes_multiline('"', &mut out) {
   306	                return out;
   307	            }
   308	            if !multi && c == '"' {
   309	                self.at += 1;
   310	                return out;
   311	            }
   312	            if !multi && c == '\n' {
   313	                self.fail("line break in a one-line string");
   314	            }
   315	            self.at += 1;
   316	            if c != '\\' {
   317	                out.push(c);
   318	                continue;
   319	            }
   320	            let Some(escape) = self.peek() else {
   321	                self.fail("unterminated escape");
   322	            };
   323	            self.at += 1;
   324	            match escape {
   325	                'b' => out.push('\u{8}'),
   326	                't' => out.push('\t'),
   327	                'n' => out.push('\n'),
   328	                'f' => out.push('\u{c}'),
   329	                'r' => out.push('\r'),
   330	                'e' => out.push('\u{1b}'),
   331	                '"' => out.push('"'),
   332	                '\\' => out.push('\\'),
   333	                'u' | 'U' | 'x' => {
   334	                    let width = match escape {
   335	                        'u' => 4,
   336	                        'U' => 8,
   337	                        _ => 2,
   338	                    };
   339	                    let Some(hex) = self.text.get(self.at..self.at + width) else {
   340	                        self.fail("short unicode escape");
   341	                    };
   342	                    let hex: String = hex.iter().collect();
   343	                    let Some(decoded) = u32::from_str_radix(&hex, 16).ok().and_then(char::from_u32)
   344	                    else {
   345	                        self.fail("bad unicode escape");
   346	                    };
   347	                    out.push(decoded);
   348	                    self.at += width;
   349	                }
   350	                ' ' | '\t' | '\n' | '\r' if multi => {
   351	                    // A line-ending backslash: the break and the next line's indent go.
   352	                    while matches!(self.peek(), Some(' ' | '\t' | '\n' | '\r')) {
   353	                        self.at += 1;
   354	                    }
   355	                }
   356	                _ => self.fail("bad escape"),
   357	            }
   358	        }
   359	    }
   360	
   361	    fn literal_string(&mut self) -> String {
   362	        let multi = self.looking_at("'''");
   363	        self.at += if multi { 3 } else { 1 };
   364	        if multi {
   365	            self.skip_first_newline();
   366	        }
   367	        let mut out = String::new();
   368	        loop {
   369	            let Some(c) = self.peek() else {
   370	                self.fail("unterminated string");
   371	            };
   372	            if multi && c == '\'' && self.closes_multiline('\'', &mut out) {
   373	                return out;
   374	            }
   375	            if !multi && c == '\'' {
   376	                self.at += 1;
   377	                return out;
   378	            }
   379	            if !multi && c == '\n' {
   380	                self.fail("line break in a one-line string");
   381	            }
   382	            out.push(c);
   383	            self.at += 1;
   384	        }
   385	    }
   386	
   387	    fn value(&mut self) -> Toml {
   388	        match self.peek() {
   389	            Some('"') => Toml::Str(self.basic_string()),
   390	            Some('\'') => Toml::Str(self.literal_string()),
   391	            Some('[') => {
   392	                self.at += 1;
   393	                let mut items = Vec::new();
   394	                loop {
   395	                    self.blank();
   396	                    if self.peek() == Some(']') {
   397	                        self.at += 1;
   398	                        return Toml::Array(items);
   399	                    }
   400	                    items.push(self.value());
   401	                    self.blank();
   402	                    match self.peek() {
   403	                        Some(',') => self.at += 1,
   404	                        Some(']') => {}
   405	                        _ => self.fail("expected `,` or `]`"),
   406	                    }
   407	                }
   408	            }
   409	            Some('{') => {
   410	                self.at += 1;
   411	                let mut table = Vec::new();
   412	                loop {
   413	                    self.blank();
   414	                    if self.peek() == Some('}') {
   415	                        self.at += 1;
   416	                        return Toml::Table(table);
   417	                    }
   418	                    let key = self.key();
   419	                    self.expect('=');
   420	                    self.spaces();
   421	                    let value = self.value();
   422	                    insert(&mut table, &key, value);
   423	                    self.blank();
   424	                    match self.peek() {
   425	                        Some(',') => self.at += 1,
   426	                        Some('}') => {}
   427	                        _ => self.fail("expected `,` or `}`"),
   428	                    }
   429	                }
   430	            }

   433	                while !matches!(
   434	                    self.peek(),
   435	                    None | Some(',' | ']' | '}' | '\n' | '\r' | '#')
   436	                ) {
   437	                    self.at += 1;
   438	                }
   439	                if start == self.at {
   440	                    self.fail("expected a value");

   442	                Toml::Other
   443	            }
   444	            None => self.fail("expected a value"),
   445	        }

   448	
   449	fn parse_toml(text: &str) -> Toml {
   450	    let mut parser = TomlParser {
   451	        // Cargo accepts a file that starts with a byte order mark.
   452	        text: text
   453	            .strip_prefix('\u{feff}')
   454	            .unwrap_or(text)
   455	            .chars()
   456	            .collect(),
   457	        at: 0,
   458	    };
   459	    let mut root = Vec::new();
   460	    let mut current: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
   461	    loop {
   462	        parser.blank();
   463	        match parser.peek() {
   464	            None => return Toml::Table(root),
   465	            Some('[') => {
   466	                parser.at += 1;
   467	                let array = parser.peek() == Some('[');
   468	                if array {
   469	                    parser.at += 1;
   470	                }
   471	                let key = parser.key();
   472	                parser.expect(']');
   473	                if array {
   474	                    parser.expect(']');
   475	                    let (last, parents) = key.split_last().expect("a header has a key");
   476	                    let parent = table_at(&mut root, parents);
   477	                    match parent.iter_mut().find(|(name, _)| name == last) {
   478	                        Some((_, Toml::Array(items))) => items.push(Toml::Table(Vec::new())),
   479	                        Some(_) => parser.fail("`[[…]]` over a key that is not an array"),
   480	                        None => {
   481	                            parent.push((last.clone(), Toml::Array(vec![Toml::Table(Vec::new())])))
   482	                        }
   483	                    }
   484	                } else {
   485	                    table_at(&mut root, &key);
   486	                }
   487	                current = key;
   488	            }
   489	            Some(_) => {
   490	                let key = parser.key();
   491	                parser.expect('=');
   492	                parser.spaces();
   493	                let value = parser.value();
   494	                insert(table_at(&mut root, &current), &key, value);
   495	            }
   496	        }
   497	        parser.spaces();
   498	        if !matches!(parser.peek(), None | Some('\n' | '\r' | '#')) {
   499	            parser.fail("expected the end of the line");
   500	        }
   501	    }
   502	}
   503	
   504	// --- The manifest -------------------------------------------------------------
   505	
   506	/// The tables a dependency can be declared in, at the root or under
   507	/// `target.<cfg>`. Cargo also takes the `_` spellings.
   508	const DEPENDENCY_KINDS: &[&str] = &[
   509	    "dependencies",
   510	    "dev-dependencies",
   511	    "dev_dependencies",
   512	    "build-dependencies",
   513	    "build_dependencies",
   514	];
   515	
   516	/// Every root key Cargo.toml may hold, so a section this file does not read
   517	/// fails `the_readers_see_every_section_they_check` instead of passing unread.
   518	const KNOWN_ROOT_KEYS: &[&str] = &[
   519	    "cargo-features",
   520	    "package",
   521	    "lib",
   522	    "bin",
   523	    "example",
   524	    "test",
   525	    "bench",
   526	    "features",
   527	    "dependencies",
   528	    "dev-dependencies",
   529	    "dev_dependencies",
   530	    "build-dependencies",
   531	    "build_dependencies",
   532	    "target",
   533	    "patch",
   534	    "replace",
   535	    "badges",
   536	    "lints",
   537	    "profile",
   538	    "workspace",
   539	];
   540	
   541	/// One dependency as the manifest declares it.
   542	struct Dep {
   543	    /// Where it is declared, as a dotted table name.
   544	    table: String,
   545	    /// The key it is declared under (for `[replace]`, the name before `:`).
   546	    name: String,
   547	    /// `package = "…"`: the crate it really is, when renamed.
   548	    package: Option<String>,
   549	    path: Option<String>,
   550	    /// `workspace = true`: the real spec is the workspace's entry of this name.
   551	    /// (Cargo refuses `workspace = false`, so the key alone says it.)
   552	    inherited: bool,
   553	}
   554	
   555	/// The crate a `[replace]` key names. Cargo takes a package ID spec there:
   556	/// `name`, `name:version` or `name@version`, optionally after a source URL and
   557	/// `#`; a URL whose fragment is only a version (`url#1.0.0`), or that has no
   558	/// fragment, names the last segment of its path.
   559	fn spec_name(spec: &str) -> String {
   560	    let (url, rest) = match spec.split_once('#') {
   561	        Some((url, rest)) => (Some(url), rest),
   562	        None if spec.contains("://") => (Some(spec), ""),
   563	        None => (None, spec),
   564	    };
   565	    let rest = if url.is_some() {
   566	        url_text(rest)
   567	    } else {
   568	        rest.to_string()
   569	    };
   570	    let name = rest.split(['@', ':']).next().unwrap_or_default();
   571	    match url {
   572	        Some(url) if name.is_empty() || name.starts_with(|c: char| c.is_ascii_digit()) => {
   573	            let last = url_path_segments(url).pop().unwrap_or_default();
   574	            last.strip_suffix(".git").unwrap_or(&last).to_string()
   575	        }
   576	        _ => name.to_string(),
   577	    }
   578	}
   579	
   580	/// A URL as the WHATWG parser Cargo uses reads it: tabs and newlines anywhere
   581	/// are dropped, and leading or trailing spaces and control characters trimmed.
   582	fn url_text(url: &str) -> String {
   583	    url.trim_matches(|c: char| c <= ' ')
   584	        .chars()
   585	        .filter(|c| !matches!(c, '\t' | '\n' | '\r'))
   586	        .collect()
   587	}
   588	
   589	/// The path segments of a URL as that parser leaves them: `\` separates them
   590	/// like `/`, `.` segments (also written `%2e`) are dropped and `..` ones remove
   591	/// the segment before, and a trailing `/` adds no segment of its own.
   592	fn url_path_segments(url: &str) -> Vec<String> {
   593	    let url = url_text(url).replace('\\', "/");
   594	    let url = url.split(['?', '#']).next().unwrap_or_default();
   595	    let path = url.split_once("://").map_or(url, |(_, rest)| {
   596	        rest.split_once('/').map_or("", |(_, path)| path)
   597	    });
   598	    let mut segments: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
   599	    for segment in path.split('/') {
   600	        match segment.to_ascii_lowercase().replace("%2e", ".").as_str() {
   601	            "" | "." => {}
   602	            ".." => {
   603	                segments.pop();
   604	            }
   605	            _ => segments.push(segment.to_string()),
   606	        }
   607	    }
   608	    segments
   609	}
   610	
   611	/// Where inherited dependency specs live. The room is its own workspace root,
   612	/// so this is in its own manifest.
   613	const WORKSPACE_DEPENDENCIES: &str = "workspace.dependencies";
   614	
   615	/// Every dependency in every dependency table, the workspace's included.
   616	fn manifest_deps(manifest: &Toml) -> Vec<Dep> {
   617	    let mut deps = Vec::new();
   618	    let mut read_table = |table: String, entries: &[(String, Toml)]| {
   619	        for (key, spec) in entries {
   620	            let field = |name: &str| spec.get(name).and_then(Toml::str).map(String::from);
   621	            deps.push(Dep {
   622	                table: table.clone(),
   623	                name: if table == "replace" {
   624	                    spec_name(key)
   625	                } else {
   626	                    key.clone()
   627	                },
   628	                package: field("package"),
   629	                path: field("path"),
   630	                inherited: spec.get("workspace").is_some(),
   631	            });
   632	        }
   633	    };
   634	    if let Some(table) = manifest
   635	        .get("workspace")
   636	        .and_then(|workspace| workspace.get("dependencies"))
   637	    {
   638	        read_table(WORKSPACE_DEPENDENCIES.to_string(), table.entries());
   639	    }
   640	    for kind in DEPENDENCY_KINDS {
   641	        if let Some(table) = manifest.get(kind) {
   642	            read_table(kind.to_string(), table.entries());
   643	        }
   644	    }
   645	    for (cfg, target) in manifest.get("target").map_or(&[][..], Toml::entries) {
   646	        for kind in DEPENDENCY_KINDS {
   647	            if let Some(table) = target.get(kind) {
   648	                read_table(format!("target.{cfg}.{kind}"), table.entries());
   649	            }
   650	        }
   651	    }
   652	    for (source, table) in manifest.get("patch").map_or(&[][..], Toml::entries) {
   653	        read_table(format!("patch.{source}"), table.entries());
   654	    }
   655	    if let Some(table) = manifest.get("replace") {
   656	        read_table("replace".to_string(), table.entries());
   657	    }
   658	    deps
   659	}
   660	
   661	/// What is wrong with a manifest that sits in `crate_dir`.
   662	fn manifest_violations(crate_dir: &Path, manifest: &str) -> Vec<String> {
   663	    let pipeline = normalize(&crate_dir.join("..").join("pipeline"));
   664	    let mut violations = Vec::new();
   665	    let deps = manifest_deps(&parse_toml(manifest));
   666	    let in_workspace: BTreeSet<&str> = deps
   667	        .iter()
   668	        .filter(|dep| dep.table == WORKSPACE_DEPENDENCIES)
   669	        .map(|dep| dep.name.as_str())
   670	        .collect();
   671	    for dep in &deps {
   672	        let at = format!("Cargo.toml [{}] {}", dep.table, dep.name);
   673	        // Its spec is checked where it is written. One this manifest does not
   674	        // write comes from a workspace above the room that this test cannot read.
   675	        if dep.inherited && !in_workspace.contains(dep.name.as_str()) {
   676	            violations.push(format!(
   677	                "{at}: inherits from a workspace outside the room's manifest"
   678	            ));
   679	        }
   680	    }
   681	    for dep in deps {
   682	        let at = format!("Cargo.toml [{}] {}", dep.table, dep.name);
   683	        if denied(&dep.name) {
   684	            violations.push(format!("{at}: denied crate name"));
   685	        }
   686	        if let Some(package) = dep.package.filter(|package| denied(package)) {
   687	            violations.push(format!("{at}: renames denied crate `{package}`"));
   688	        }
   689	        if let Some(path) = dep.path {
   690	            if normalize(&crate_dir.join(&path)).starts_with(&pipeline) {
   691	                violations.push(format!("{at}: path `{path}` points into pipeline/"));
   692	            }
   693	        }
   694	    }
   695	    violations
   696	}
   697	
   698	// --- The lock -------------------------------------------------------------------
   699	
   700	struct Package {
   701	    name: String,
   702	    version: String,
   703	    /// `None` for a path package: the room itself, or a path dependency.
   704	    source: Option<String>,
   705	    /// As the lock writes them: `name`, `name version` or `name version (source)`.
   706	    /// A `replace = "name version (source)"` field, which `[replace]` writes on
   707	    /// the package it redirects, is one more: Cargo builds that package instead.
   708	    deps: Vec<String>,
   709	}
   710	
   711	fn lock_packages(lock: &str) -> Vec<Package> {
   712	    let lock = parse_toml(lock);
   713	    let text = |package: &Toml, key: &str| package.get(key).and_then(Toml::str).map(String::from);
   714	    lock.get("package")
   715	        .map_or(&[][..], Toml::items)
   716	        .iter()
   717	        .map(|package| Package {
   718	            name: text(package, "name").unwrap_or_default(),
   719	            version: text(package, "version").unwrap_or_default(),
   720	            source: text(package, "source"),
   721	            deps: package
   722	                .get("dependencies")
   723	                .map_or(&[][..], Toml::items)
   724	                .iter()
   725	                .filter_map(Toml::str)
   726	                .map(String::from)
   727	                .chain(text(package, "replace"))
   728	                .collect(),
   729	        })
   730	        .collect()
   731	}
   732	
   733	/// A lock source without the commit a git source is pinned to. Cargo writes a
   734	/// package's source as `git+URL?query#commit` and a dependency line's as
   735	/// `(git+URL?query)`; the URL and its `?branch=`/`?tag=`/`?rev=` still tell
   736	/// two git sources apart.
   737	fn unpinned(source: &str) -> &str {
   738	    source.split_once('#').map_or(source, |(source, _)| source)
   739	}
   740	
   741	/// The packages a lock entry's dependency line can mean. The lock adds the
   742	/// version, then the source, only when the shorter form would be ambiguous.
   743	fn resolve<'a>(packages: &'a [Package], dep: &str) -> Vec<usize> {
   744	    let (spec, source) = match dep.split_once(" (") {
   745	        // Only the closing delimiter: a source URL may end in `)` itself.
   746	        Some((spec, source)) => (spec, Some(source.strip_suffix(')').unwrap_or(source))),
   747	        None => (dep, None),
   748	    };
   749	    let mut words = spec.split_whitespace();
   750	    let name = words.next().unwrap_or_default();
   751	    let version = words.next();
   752	    (0..packages.len())
   753	        .filter(|&index| {
   754	            let candidate: &'a Package = &packages[index];
   755	            candidate.name == name
   756	                && version.is_none_or(|version| candidate.version == version)
   757	                && source.is_none_or(|source| {
   758	                    candidate.source.as_deref().map(unpinned) == Some(unpinned(source))
   759	                })
   760	        })
   761	        .collect()
   762	}
   763	
   764	/// Every package the room reaches in its lock, the room included.
   765	fn room_subgraph(packages: &[Package]) -> Vec<&Package> {
   766	    let root = packages
   767	        .iter()
   768	        .position(|package| package.name == "room" && package.source.is_none())
   769	        .expect("the lock holds the room crate itself");
   770	    let mut seen = BTreeSet::new();
   771	    let mut stack = vec![root];
   772	    while let Some(index) = stack.pop() {
   773	        if !seen.insert(index) {
   774	            continue;
   775	        }
   776	        let package = &packages[index];
   777	        for dep in &package.deps {
   778	            let found = resolve(packages, dep);
   779	            assert!(
   780	                !found.is_empty(),
   781	                "Cargo.lock lists `{dep}` under {} but holds no such package",
   782	                package.name
   783	            );
   784	            stack.extend(found);
   785	        }
   786	    }
   787	    seen.into_iter().map(|index| &packages[index]).collect()
   788	}
   789	
   790	fn lock_violations(lock: &str) -> Vec<String> {
   791	    let packages = lock_packages(lock);
   792	    let mut violations = Vec::new();
   793	    for package in room_subgraph(&packages) {
   794	        let at = format!("Cargo.lock {} {}", package.name, package.version);
   795	        if denied(&package.name) {
   796	            violations.push(format!("{at}: denied crate in the room's subgraph"));
   797	        }
   798	        if package.source.is_none() && package.name != "room" {
   799	            violations.push(format!("{at}: a path package other than the room"));
   800	        }
   801	    }
   802	    violations
   803	}
   804	
   805	// --- The source -----------------------------------------------------------------
   806	//
   807	// A tokenizer that knows what Rust does not read as code: line and nested block
   808	// comments, string, byte and C string literals (raw ones too), character
   809	// literals and lifetimes. What is left is identifiers (raw ones unwrapped),
   810	// `::`, braces, `;`, `,` and single punctuation, which is all the import reader
   811	// needs.
   812	
   813	#[derive(Debug, PartialEq)]
   814	enum Tok {
   815	    /// An identifier or keyword; `true` when written raw (`r#name`), which is
   816	    /// never a keyword.
   817	    Ident(String, bool),
   818	    PathSep,
   819	    Open,
   820	    Close,
   821	    Semi,
   822	    Comma,
   823	    /// Any other single character: `#`, `!`, `[`, `]`, `(`, `<`, …
   824	    Punct(char),
   825	}
   826	
   827	fn is_ident_char(c: char) -> bool {
   828	    c.is_alphanumeric() || c == '_'
   829	}
   830	
   831	/// Past a `"…"` body whose opening quote is just before `at`.
   832	fn skip_quoted(text: &[char], mut at: usize) -> usize {
   833	    while at < text.len() {
   834	        match text[at] {
   835	            '\\' => at += 2,
   836	            '"' => return at + 1,
   837	            _ => at += 1,
   838	        }
   839	    }
   840	    at
   841	}
   842	
   843	/// Past a raw string body whose opening `"` is just before `at`.
   844	fn skip_raw(text: &[char], mut at: usize, hashes: usize) -> usize {
   845	    while at < text.len() {
   846	        if text[at] == '"' && (1..=hashes).all(|k| text.get(at + k) == Some(&'#')) {
   847	            return at + 1 + hashes;
   848	        }
   849	        at += 1;
   850	    }
   851	    at
   852	}
   853	
   854	/// Past a character literal or a lifetime that starts with the `'` at `at`.
   855	fn skip_tick(text: &[char], at: usize) -> usize {
   856	    if text.get(at + 1) == Some(&'\\') {
   857	        let mut end = at + 3;
   858	        while end < text.len() && text[end] != '\'' {
   859	            end += 1;
   860	        }
   861	        return end + 1;
   862	    }
   863	    if text.get(at + 2) == Some(&'\'') {
   864	        return at + 3;
   865	    }
   866	    let mut end = at + 1;
   867	    while end < text.len() && is_ident_char(text[end]) {
   868	        end += 1;
   869	    }
   870	    end
   871	}
   872	
   873	fn tokens(source: &str) -> Vec<Tok> {
   874	    let text: Vec<char> = source.chars().collect();
   875	    let mut out = Vec::new();
   876	    let mut at = 0;
   877	    while at < text.len() {
   878	        let c = text[at];
   879	        let next = text.get(at + 1).copied();
   880	        if c.is_whitespace() {
   881	            at += 1;
   882	        } else if c == '/' && next == Some('/') {
   883	            while at < text.len() && text[at] != '\n' {
   884	                at += 1;
   885	            }
   886	        } else if c == '/' && next == Some('*') {
   887	            let mut depth = 0;
   888	            while at < text.len() {
   889	                if text[at] == '/' && text.get(at + 1) == Some(&'*') {
   890	                    depth += 1;
   891	                    at += 2;
   892	                } else if text[at] == '*' && text.get(at + 1) == Some(&'/') {
   893	                    depth -= 1;
   894	                    at += 2;
   895	                    if depth == 0 {
   896	                        break;
   897	                    }
   898	                } else {
   899	                    at += 1;
   900	                }

exec
/bin/zsh -lc "nl -ba popquiz/tests/dependencies.rs | sed -n '901,1450p'" in /Users/michellerojas/rust-nyc-pop-quiz-worktrees/pq-47-room-dep-denylist
 succeeded in 0ms:
   901	            }
   902	        } else if c == '"' {
   903	            at = skip_quoted(&text, at + 1);
   904	        } else if c == '\'' {
   905	            at = skip_tick(&text, at);
   906	        } else if c.is_alphabetic() || c == '_' {
   907	            let start = at;
   908	            while at < text.len() && is_ident_char(text[at]) {
   909	                at += 1;
   910	            }
   911	            let word: String = text[start..at].iter().collect();
   912	            let after = text.get(at).copied();
   913	            if matches!(word.as_str(), "b" | "c") && after == Some('"') {
   914	                at = skip_quoted(&text, at + 1);
   915	                continue;
   916	            }
   917	            if word == "b" && after == Some('\'') {
   918	                at = skip_tick(&text, at);
   919	                continue;
   920	            }
   921	            if matches!(word.as_str(), "r" | "br" | "cr") && matches!(after, Some('"' | '#')) {
   922	                let mut end = at;
   923	                while text.get(end) == Some(&'#') {
   924	                    end += 1;
   925	                }
   926	                let hashes = end - at;
   927	                if text.get(end) == Some(&'"') {
   928	                    at = skip_raw(&text, end + 1, hashes);
   929	                    continue;
   930	                }
   931	                if word == "r"
   932	                    && hashes == 1
   933	                    && text
   934	                        .get(end)
   935	                        .is_some_and(|c| c.is_alphabetic() || *c == '_')
   936	                {
   937	                    let start = end;
   938	                    while end < text.len() && is_ident_char(text[end]) {
   939	                        end += 1;
   940	                    }
   941	                    out.push(Tok::Ident(text[start..end].iter().collect(), true));
   942	                    at = end;
   943	                    continue;
   944	                }
   945	            }
   946	            out.push(Tok::Ident(word, false));
   947	        } else if c.is_ascii_digit() {
   948	            while at < text.len() && is_ident_char(text[at]) {
   949	                at += 1;
   950	            }
   951	        } else {
   952	            at += 1;
   953	            out.push(match c {
   954	                ':' if next == Some(':') => {
   955	                    at += 1;
   956	                    Tok::PathSep
   957	                }
   958	                '{' => Tok::Open,
   959	                '}' => Tok::Close,
   960	                ';' => Tok::Semi,
   961	                ',' => Tok::Comma,
   962	                _ => Tok::Punct(c),
   963	            });
   964	        }
   965	    }
   966	    out
   967	}
   968	
   969	fn keyword(token: Option<&Tok>, word: &str) -> bool {
   970	    matches!(token, Some(Tok::Ident(name, false)) if name == word)
   971	}
   972	
   973	/// The root of each path in a use tree, and whether it was written absolute
   974	/// (`::name`, which in Rust 2018 and later is always an external crate).
   975	fn use_roots(tree: &[Tok], absolute: bool) -> Vec<(String, bool)> {
   976	    match tree.first() {
   977	        Some(Tok::PathSep) => use_roots(&tree[1..], true),
   978	        Some(Tok::Open) => {
   979	            let mut out = Vec::new();
   980	            let mut depth = 0;
   981	            let mut start = 1;
   982	            for (index, token) in tree.iter().enumerate().skip(1) {
   983	                match token {
   984	                    Tok::Open => depth += 1,
   985	                    Tok::Close if depth == 0 => {
   986	                        out.extend(use_roots(&tree[start..index], absolute));
   987	                        break;
   988	                    }
   989	                    Tok::Close => depth -= 1,
   990	                    Tok::Comma if depth == 0 => {
   991	                        out.extend(use_roots(&tree[start..index], absolute));
   992	                        start = index + 1;
   993	                    }
   994	                    _ => {}
   995	                }
   996	            }
   997	            out
   998	        }
   999	        Some(Tok::Ident(name, _)) => vec![(name.clone(), absolute)],
  1000	        _ => Vec::new(),
  1001	    }
  1002	}
  1003	
  1004	/// One crate-or-module root a file imports.
  1005	struct Import {
  1006	    /// The scope the import sits in (see [`FileImports::parent`]).
  1007	    scope: usize,
  1008	    root: String,
  1009	    /// Written `::root`, or by `extern crate`: always an external crate.
  1010	    external: bool,
  1011	    keyword: &'static str,
  1012	}
  1013	
  1014	/// A file's imports and the module names it declares, by scope.
  1015	struct FileImports {
  1016	    found: Vec<Import>,
  1017	    /// `(scope, name)` for each `mod name;` or `mod name { … }`.
  1018	    declared: BTreeSet<(usize, String)>,
  1019	    /// Each scope's enclosing scope. Scope 0 is the file; every brace opens
  1020	    /// one more. A block sees the names its enclosing blocks declare, up to its
  1021	    /// module, so a block's parent is the scope around it. A module (the file,
  1022	    /// or an inline `mod name { … }`) has none: Rust does not look past it
  1023	    /// without `super::`.
  1024	    parent: Vec<Option<usize>>,
  1025	    /// Each inline module's scope, and the `(scope, name)` that declares it,
  1026	    /// whether or not the declaration counts.
  1027	    modules: BTreeMap<usize, (usize, String)>,
  1028	    /// The declarations written `mod name;`, which load a file.
  1029	    loads: BTreeSet<(usize, String)>,
  1030	    /// The file starts `#![cfg(…)]` (or `cfg_attr`, `path`), so the module it
  1031	    /// holds may be absent.
  1032	    conditional: bool,
  1033	}
  1034	
  1035	impl FileImports {
  1036	    /// Whether `name` is a module that `scope` can see: declared in it, or in a
  1037	    /// scope around it within the same module.
  1038	    fn declares(&self, scope: usize, name: &str) -> bool {
  1039	        let mut at = Some(scope);
  1040	        while let Some(scope) = at {
  1041	            if self.declared.contains(&(scope, name.to_string())) {
  1042	                return true;
  1043	            }
  1044	            at = self.parent[scope];
  1045	        }
  1046	        false
  1047	    }
  1048	}
  1049	
  1050	/// Which tokens are a macro's input: inside `name!(…)`, `name![…]`,
  1051	/// `name!{…}`, or the body of `macro_rules! name {…}`.
  1052	fn macro_inputs(tokens: &[Tok]) -> Vec<bool> {
  1053	    let mut inside = vec![false; tokens.len()];
  1054	    for at in 0..tokens.len() {
  1055	        if !matches!(tokens[at], Tok::Ident(..)) || tokens.get(at + 1) != Some(&Tok::Punct('!')) {
  1056	            continue;
  1057	        }
  1058	        let mut open = at + 2;
  1059	        if keyword(tokens.get(at), "macro_rules")
  1060	            && matches!(tokens.get(open), Some(Tok::Ident(..)))
  1061	        {
  1062	            open += 1;
  1063	        }
  1064	        let close = match tokens.get(open) {
  1065	            Some(Tok::Punct('(')) => Tok::Punct(')'),
  1066	            Some(Tok::Punct('[')) => Tok::Punct(']'),
  1067	            Some(Tok::Open) => Tok::Close,
  1068	            _ => continue,
  1069	        };
  1070	        let opening = &tokens[open];
  1071	        let mut depth = 0;
  1072	        for index in open..tokens.len() {
  1073	            if tokens[index] == *opening {
  1074	                depth += 1;
  1075	            } else if tokens[index] == close {
  1076	                depth -= 1;
  1077	                if depth == 0 {
  1078	                    break;
  1079	                }
  1080	            }
  1081	            inside[index] = true;
  1082	        }
  1083	    }
  1084	    inside
  1085	}
  1086	
  1087	fn imports(source: &str) -> FileImports {
  1088	    let tokens = tokens(source);
  1089	    let in_macro = macro_inputs(&tokens);
  1090	    let mut file = FileImports {
  1091	        found: Vec::new(),
  1092	        declared: BTreeSet::new(),
  1093	        parent: vec![None],
  1094	        modules: BTreeMap::new(),
  1095	        loads: BTreeSet::new(),
  1096	        conditional: false,
  1097	    };
  1098	    let mut scopes = vec![0];
  1099	    // The next item carries a `#[cfg…]`, `#[cfg_attr…]` or `#[path…]`. Only an
  1100	    // unconditional `mod` makes a `use` of its name local: a conditional one
  1101	    // may be absent exactly when the `use` is compiled.
  1102	    let mut conditional = false;
  1103	    let mut at = 0;
  1104	    while at < tokens.len() {
  1105	        let here = *scopes.last().expect("the file scope is never popped");
  1106	        if let Some((end, inner, gated)) = attribute(&tokens, at) {
  1107	            if gated && !inner {
  1108	                conditional = true;
  1109	            } else if gated && here == 0 {
  1110	                // An inner `#![cfg…]` gates the module it sits in: this file's,
  1111	                file.conditional = true;
  1112	            } else if let Some(declaration) = file.modules.get(&here).filter(|_| gated) {
  1113	                // or an inline one's.
  1114	                file.declared.remove(declaration);
  1115	            }
  1116	            at = end;
  1117	            continue;
  1118	        }
  1119	        if keyword(tokens.get(at), "mod") {
  1120	            // In a macro's input a `mod` is only tokens, whatever the macro
  1121	            // makes of them.
  1122	            let counts = !conditional && !in_macro[at];
  1123	            conditional = false;
  1124	            if let Some(Tok::Ident(name, _)) = tokens.get(at + 1) {
  1125	                let declaration = (here, name.clone());
  1126	                match tokens.get(at + 2) {
  1127	                    Some(Tok::Semi) => {
  1128	                        if counts {
  1129	                            file.loads.insert(declaration.clone());
  1130	                            file.declared.insert(declaration);
  1131	                        }
  1132	                        at += 3;
  1133	                        continue;
  1134	                    }
  1135	                    Some(Tok::Open) => {
  1136	                        let scope = file.parent.len();
  1137	                        if counts {
  1138	                            file.declared.insert(declaration.clone());
  1139	                        }
  1140	                        file.modules.insert(scope, declaration);
  1141	                        scopes.push(scope);
  1142	                        file.parent.push(None);
  1143	                        at += 3;
  1144	                        continue;
  1145	                    }
  1146	                    _ => {}
  1147	                }
  1148	            }
  1149	        } else if keyword(tokens.get(at), "use")
  1150	            && !matches!(tokens.get(at + 1), Some(Tok::Punct(_)))
  1151	        {
  1152	            conditional = false;
  1153	            // `use<'a>` in `impl Trait + use<'a>` is not an import, and is the
  1154	            // only `use` followed by punctuation.
  1155	            let end = tokens[at + 1..]
  1156	                .iter()
  1157	                .position(|token| *token == Tok::Semi)
  1158	                .map_or(tokens.len(), |offset| at + 1 + offset);
  1159	            for (root, external) in use_roots(&tokens[at + 1..end], false) {
  1160	                file.found.push(Import {
  1161	                    scope: here,
  1162	                    root,
  1163	                    external,
  1164	                    keyword: "use",
  1165	                });
  1166	            }
  1167	            at = end + 1;
  1168	            continue;
  1169	        } else if keyword(tokens.get(at), "extern") && keyword(tokens.get(at + 1), "crate") {
  1170	            conditional = false;
  1171	            if let Some(Tok::Ident(name, _)) = tokens.get(at + 2) {
  1172	                file.found.push(Import {
  1173	                    scope: here,
  1174	                    root: name.clone(),
  1175	                    external: true,
  1176	                    keyword: "extern crate",
  1177	                });
  1178	                at += 3;
  1179	                continue;
  1180	            }
  1181	        } else if tokens[at] == Tok::Open {
  1182	            conditional = false;
  1183	            scopes.push(file.parent.len());
  1184	            file.parent.push(Some(here));
  1185	        } else if tokens[at] == Tok::Close {
  1186	            conditional = false;
  1187	            if scopes.len() > 1 {
  1188	                scopes.pop();
  1189	            }
  1190	        } else if matches!(tokens[at], Tok::Semi | Tok::Comma) {
  1191	            conditional = false;
  1192	        }
  1193	        at += 1;
  1194	    }
  1195	    file
  1196	}
  1197	
  1198	/// The attribute starting at `at` (`#[…]`, or inner `#![…]`): where it ends,
  1199	/// whether it is inner, and whether it can make its item absent or come from
  1200	/// another file (`cfg`, `cfg_attr`, `path`).
  1201	fn attribute(tokens: &[Tok], at: usize) -> Option<(usize, bool, bool)> {
  1202	    if tokens.get(at) != Some(&Tok::Punct('#')) {
  1203	        return None;
  1204	    }
  1205	    let inner = tokens.get(at + 1) == Some(&Tok::Punct('!'));
  1206	    let open = at + 1 + usize::from(inner);
  1207	    if tokens.get(open) != Some(&Tok::Punct('[')) {
  1208	        return None;
  1209	    }
  1210	    let mut depth = 0;
  1211	    for (index, token) in tokens.iter().enumerate().skip(open) {
  1212	        match token {
  1213	            Tok::Punct('[') => depth += 1,
  1214	            Tok::Punct(']') => {
  1215	                depth -= 1;
  1216	                if depth == 0 {
  1217	                    // Raw or not: `#[r#cfg(…)]` is `#[cfg(…)]`.
  1218	                    let conditional = tokens[open..index].iter().any(|token| {
  1219	                        matches!(token, Tok::Ident(name, _)
  1220	                            if matches!(name.as_str(), "cfg" | "cfg_attr" | "path"))
  1221	                    });
  1222	                    return Some((index + 1, inner, conditional));
  1223	                }
  1224	            }
  1225	            _ => {}
  1226	        }
  1227	    }
  1228	    None
  1229	}
  1230	
  1231	/// What is wrong with one source file, and the denied names it was allowed to
  1232	/// `use` because its own module declares them.
  1233	///
  1234	/// A `use` root that its scope can see declared with `mod` is that module,
  1235	/// which is how Rust resolves it: `server/routes/mod.rs` declares
  1236	/// `mod pipeline;` (the room's admin route for the pipeline's pushes) and then
  1237	/// `use pipeline::admin_routes;`. A `mod` inside a block is seen only inside
  1238	/// that block, and a conditional `mod` (see [`imports`]) is not counted, nor
  1239	/// (see [`scan_files`]) is a `mod name;` whose file gates itself. An absolute
  1240	/// `::name` and `extern crate` always name a crate, so they get no such reading.
  1241	fn file_violations(file: &str, scanned: &FileImports) -> (Vec<String>, BTreeSet<String>) {
  1242	    let mut violations = Vec::new();
  1243	    let mut local = BTreeSet::new();
  1244	    for import in &scanned.found {
  1245	        if !denied(&import.root) {
  1246	            continue;
  1247	        }
  1248	        if !import.external && scanned.declares(import.scope, &import.root) {
  1249	            local.insert(import.root.clone());
  1250	            continue;
  1251	        }
  1252	        violations.push(format!(
  1253	            "{file}: `{}` of denied crate `{}`",
  1254	            import.keyword, import.root
  1255	        ));
  1256	    }
  1257	    (violations, local)
  1258	}
  1259	
  1260	/// One file on its own, as the planted cases use it.
  1261	fn source_violations(file: &str, source: &str) -> (Vec<String>, BTreeSet<String>) {
  1262	    file_violations(file, &imports(source))
  1263	}
  1264	
  1265	struct SourceScan {
  1266	    files: usize,
  1267	    imports: usize,
  1268	    violations: Vec<String>,
  1269	    local: BTreeSet<String>,
  1270	}
  1271	
  1272	/// The crate roots: the library, each binary the manifest names, and Cargo's
  1273	/// default binary paths. A `mod name;` in a root loads from the root's own
  1274	/// directory, as in a `mod.rs`.
  1275	fn crate_roots(manifest: &Toml) -> BTreeSet<PathBuf> {
  1276	    let path = |target: &Toml| target.get("path").and_then(Toml::str).map(clean);
  1277	    let mut roots: BTreeSet<PathBuf> = ["src/lib.rs", "src/main.rs"]
  1278	        .into_iter()
  1279	        .map(PathBuf::from)
  1280	        .collect();
  1281	    roots.extend(manifest.get("lib").and_then(path));
  1282	    roots.extend(
  1283	        manifest
  1284	            .get("bin")
  1285	            .map_or(&[][..], Toml::items)
  1286	            .iter()
  1287	            .filter_map(path),
  1288	    );
  1289	    roots
  1290	}
  1291	
  1292	/// A manifest path as the scan names files: relative, without `./`.
  1293	fn clean(path: &str) -> PathBuf {
  1294	    Path::new(path)
  1295	        .components()
  1296	        .filter(|component| *component != std::path::Component::CurDir)
  1297	        .collect()
  1298	}
  1299	
  1300	/// Whether the file at `path` is a crate root: named in `roots`, or one of
  1301	/// Cargo's automatic binaries, `src/bin/name.rs` or `src/bin/name/main.rs`.
  1302	fn is_root(path: &Path, roots: &BTreeSet<PathBuf>) -> bool {
  1303	    let bin = Path::new("src/bin");
  1304	    roots.contains(path)
  1305	        || path.parent() == Some(bin)
  1306	        || (path.file_name() == Some("main.rs".as_ref())
  1307	            && path.parent().and_then(Path::parent) == Some(bin))
  1308	}
  1309	
  1310	/// The files `mod name;` at `scope` in the file at `path` can load: `name.rs`
  1311	/// or `name/mod.rs`, inside each inline module around it, under the declaring
  1312	/// file's own directory (a `mod.rs` or a crate root) or, for any other file,
  1313	/// the directory named for it. `None` when an inline module around it is
  1314	/// itself conditional, which may carry a `#[path]` that moves it.
  1315	fn module_files(
  1316	    path: &Path,
  1317	    root: bool,
  1318	    file: &FileImports,
  1319	    (scope, name): &(usize, String),
  1320	) -> Option<Vec<PathBuf>> {
  1321	    let mut inline = Vec::new();
  1322	    let mut at = Some(*scope);
  1323	    while let Some(scope) = at {
  1324	        match file.modules.get(&scope) {
  1325	            Some(declaration) => {
  1326	                if !file.declared.contains(declaration) {
  1327	                    return None;
  1328	                }
  1329	                inline.push(declaration.1.clone());
  1330	                at = Some(declaration.0);
  1331	            }
  1332	            None => at = file.parent[scope],
  1333	        }
  1334	    }
  1335	    let dir = path.parent().unwrap_or(Path::new(""));
  1336	    let base = match path.file_stem() {
  1337	        Some(stem) if !root && path.file_name() != Some("mod.rs".as_ref()) => dir.join(stem),
  1338	        _ => dir.to_path_buf(),
  1339	    };
  1340	    let dir = inline
  1341	        .iter()
  1342	        .rev()
  1343	        .fold(base, |dir, module| dir.join(module));
  1344	    Some(vec![
  1345	        dir.join(format!("{name}.rs")),
  1346	        dir.join(name).join("mod.rs"),
  1347	    ])
  1348	}
  1349	
  1350	fn scan_source(crate_dir: &Path) -> SourceScan {
  1351	    let mut paths = Vec::new();
  1352	    rust_files(&crate_dir.join("src"), &mut paths);
  1353	    let files: Vec<(PathBuf, String)> = paths
  1354	        .into_iter()
  1355	        .map(|path| {
  1356	            let source = read(&path);
  1357	            (
  1358	                path.strip_prefix(crate_dir).unwrap_or(&path).to_path_buf(),
  1359	                source,
  1360	            )
  1361	        })
  1362	        .collect();
  1363	    let roots = crate_roots(&parse_toml(&read(&crate_dir.join("Cargo.toml"))));
  1364	    scan_files(&files, &roots)
  1365	}
  1366	
  1367	/// Source files as paths within the crate and their text, and the crate roots.
  1368	fn scan_files(files: &[(PathBuf, String)], roots: &BTreeSet<PathBuf>) -> SourceScan {
  1369	    let mut scanned: Vec<FileImports> = files.iter().map(|(_, source)| imports(source)).collect();
  1370	    // A module file that gates itself makes the `mod name;` that loads it
  1371	    // conditional, as does one whose file this cannot place.
  1372	    let gated: BTreeSet<PathBuf> = files
  1373	        .iter()
  1374	        .zip(&scanned)
  1375	        .filter(|(_, file)| file.conditional)
  1376	        .map(|((path, _), _)| path.clone())
  1377	        .collect();
  1378	    for ((path, _), file) in files.iter().zip(&mut scanned) {
  1379	        let conditional: Vec<(usize, String)> = file
  1380	            .loads
  1381	            .iter()
  1382	            .filter(|declaration| {
  1383	                module_files(path, is_root(path, roots), file, declaration)
  1384	                    .is_none_or(|candidates| candidates.iter().any(|path| gated.contains(path)))
  1385	            })
  1386	            .cloned()
  1387	            .collect();
  1388	        for declaration in conditional {
  1389	            file.declared.remove(&declaration);
  1390	        }
  1391	    }
  1392	    let mut scan = SourceScan {
  1393	        files: files.len(),
  1394	        imports: 0,
  1395	        violations: Vec::new(),
  1396	        local: BTreeSet::new(),
  1397	    };
  1398	    for ((path, _), file) in files.iter().zip(&scanned) {
  1399	        scan.imports += file.found.len();
  1400	        let (violations, local) = file_violations(&path.display().to_string(), file);
  1401	        scan.violations.extend(violations);
  1402	        scan.local.extend(local);
  1403	    }
  1404	    scan
  1405	}
  1406	
  1407	fn refuse(violations: &[String]) {
  1408	    assert!(
  1409	        violations.is_empty(),
  1410	        "{WHY}:\n  {}",
  1411	        violations.join("\n  ")
  1412	    );
  1413	}
  1414
