  1527	
  1528	/// The real manifest with `extra` added straight after its first `header` line.
  1529	fn planted(manifest: &str, header: &str, extra: &str) -> String {
  1530	    let line = format!("{header}\n");
  1531	    let out = manifest.replacen(&line, &format!("{line}{extra}\n"), 1);
  1532	    assert_ne!(out, manifest, "{header} is not in Cargo.toml");
  1533	    out
  1534	}
  1535	
  1536	/// The mutations named on PQ-47, and the variants a review found, planted in
  1537	/// the real text. On the real files M1 and M2 stop `cargo --locked` before any
  1538	/// test runs; here they reach the checks, so the checks stay able to refuse them.
  1539	#[test]
  1540	fn the_planted_mutations_are_refused() {
  1541	    let dir = crate_dir();
  1542	    // Without a byte order mark of its own, so a fixture can add one.
  1543	    let manifest = read(&dir.join("Cargo.toml")).replacen('\u{feff}', "", 1);
  1544	    let refused = |text: &str, expected: &str| {
  1545	        let found = manifest_violations(&dir, text);
  1546	        assert!(
  1547	            found.iter().any(|violation| violation.contains(expected)),
  1548	            "expected a violation containing `{expected}`, got {found:?}"
  1549	        );
  1550	    };
  1551	
  1552	    // M1: a path dependency on the pipeline.
  1553	    let m1 = planted(
  1554	        &manifest,
  1555	        "[dependencies]",
  1556	        "pipeline = { path = \"../pipeline\" }",
  1557	    );
  1558	    refused(&m1, "path `../pipeline` points into pipeline/");
  1559	    refused(&m1, "[dependencies] pipeline: denied crate name");
  1560	    refused(&format!("\u{feff}{m1}"), "points into pipeline/");
  1561	
  1562	    // M2: an optional, feature-gated LLM client.
  1563	    let m2 = planted(
  1564	        &planted(&manifest, "[features]", "llm = [\"dep:anthropic\"]"),
  1565	        "[dependencies]",
  1566	        "anthropic = { version = \"0.1\", optional = true }",
  1567	    );
  1568	    refused(&m2, "[dependencies] anthropic: denied crate name");
  1569	
  1570	    // Manifest variants: dotted keys, quoted keys, escaped and multi-line
  1571	    // strings, dotted and quoted headers, patch and replace.
  1572	    for (extra_header, extra, expected) in [
  1573	        (
  1574	            "[dependencies]",
  1575	            "anthropic.version = \"0.1\"\nanthropic.optional = true",
  1576	            "] anthropic: denied",
  1577	        ),
  1578	        (
  1579	            "[dependencies]",
  1580	            "client = { \"package\" = \"openai\", version = \"0.1\" }",
  1581	            "`openai`",
  1582	        ),
  1583	        (
  1584	            "[dependencies]",
  1585	            "helper = { \"path\" = \"../pipeline\" }",
  1586	            "points into pipeline/",
  1587	        ),
  1588	        (
  1589	            "[dependencies]",
  1590	            "helper = { path = '../pipeline/src' }",
  1591	            "points into pipeline/",
  1592	        ),
  1593	        (
  1594	            "[dependencies]",
  1595	            "client = { package = \"op\\u0065nai\", version = \"0.1\" }",
  1596	            "`openai`",
  1597	        ),
  1598	        (
  1599	            "[dependencies]",
  1600	            "client = { package = \"\"\"openai\"\"\", version = \"0.1\" }",
  1601	            "`openai`",
  1602	        ),
  1603	        (
  1604	            "[dev-dependencies]",
  1605	            "client = { package = \"async-openai\", version = \"0.1\" }",
  1606	            "`async-openai`",
  1607	        ),
  1608	    ] {
  1609	        refused(&planted(&manifest, extra_header, extra), expected);
  1610	    }
  1611	    for (appended, expected) in [
  1612	        (
  1613	            "[target.'cfg(unix)'.build-dependencies.helper]\npackage = \"llm\"",
  1614	            "`llm`",
  1615	        ),
  1616	        (
  1617	            "[target.'cfg(any())'.\"build-dependencies\"]\nllm = \"0.1\"",
  1618	            "] llm: denied",
  1619	        ),
  1620	        (
  1621	            "[target . 'cfg(any())' . build-dependencies]\nllm = \"0.1\"",
  1622	            "] llm: denied",
  1623	        ),
  1624	        (
  1625	            "[patch.crates-io]\nopenai = { git = \"https://example.com/x\" }",
  1626	            "[patch.crates-io] openai",
  1627	        ),
  1628	        (
  1629	            "[replace]\n\"miri:0.1.0\" = { git = \"https://example.com/x\" }",
  1630	            "[replace] miri",
  1631	        ),
  1632	        // The other package ID spec forms a `[replace]` key can take.
  1633	        (
  1634	            "[replace]\n\"openai@0.1.0\" = { git = \"https://example.com/x\" }",
  1635	            "[replace] openai",
  1636	        ),
  1637	        (
  1638	            "[replace]\n\"https://github.com/rust-lang/crates.io-index#openai@0.1.0\" = \
  1639	             { git = \"https://example.com/x\" }",
  1640	            "[replace] openai",
  1641	        ),
  1642	        (
  1643	            "[replace]\n\"registry+https://github.com/rust-lang/crates.io-index#openai:0.1.0\" = \
  1644	             { git = \"https://example.com/x\" }",
  1645	            "[replace] openai",
  1646	        ),
  1647	        (
  1648	            "[replace]\n\"https://example.com/openai#0.1.0\" = { git = \"https://example.com/x\" }",
  1649	            "[replace] openai",
  1650	        ),
  1651	        (
  1652	            "[replace]\n\"git+https://example.com/x/openai.git?branch=main\" = \
  1653	             { git = \"https://example.com/x\" }",
  1654	            "[replace] openai",
  1655	        ),
  1656	        // Spellings the URL parser Cargo uses normalizes to the same name.
  1657	        (
  1658	            "[replace]\n'https://example.com/helpers\\openai#0.1.0' = { git = \"https://example.com/x\" }",
  1659	            "[replace] openai",
  1660	        ),
  1661	        (
  1662	            "[replace]\n\"https://example.com/openai/x/..#0.1.0\" = { git = \"https://example.com/x\" }",
  1663	            "[replace] openai",
  1664	        ),
  1665	        (
  1666	            "[replace]\n\"https://example.com/openai/%2E/#0.1.0\" = { git = \"https://example.com/x\" }",
  1667	            "[replace] openai",
  1668	        ),
  1669	        (
  1670	            "[replace]\n\"https://example.com/open\\tai#0.1.0\" = { git = \"https://example.com/x\" }",
  1671	            "[replace] openai",
  1672	        ),
  1673	        (
  1674	            "[replace]\n\"https://example.com/x#open\\nai@0.1.0\" = { git = \"https://example.com/x\" }",
  1675	            "[replace] openai",
  1676	        ),
  1677	    ] {
  1678	        refused(&format!("{manifest}\n{appended}\n"), expected);
  1679	    }
  1680	
  1681	    // Workspace inheritance: the spec is in `[workspace.dependencies]`, or in a
  1682	    // workspace above the room that this test cannot read.
  1683	    let inheriting = planted(
  1684	        &manifest,
  1685	        "[dependencies]",
  1686	        "client = { workspace = true, optional = true }",
  1687	    );
  1688	    refused(
  1689	        &format!(
  1690	            "{inheriting}\n[workspace.dependencies]\n\
  1691	             client = {{ package = \"openai\", version = \"0.1\" }}\n"
  1692	        ),
  1693	        "[workspace.dependencies] client: renames denied crate `openai`",
  1694	    );
  1695	    refused(
  1696	        &format!("{manifest}\n[workspace.dependencies]\nhelper = {{ path = \"../pipeline\" }}\n"),
  1697	        "[workspace.dependencies] helper: path `../pipeline` points into pipeline/",
  1698	    );
  1699	    // A name of its own, so a manifest that already inherits `client` from its
  1700	    // own workspace still shows this case.
  1701	    refused(
  1702	        &planted(
  1703	            &manifest,
  1704	            "[dependencies]",
  1705	            "pq47-inherited = { workspace = true }",
  1706	        ),
  1707	        "[dependencies] pq47-inherited: inherits from a workspace outside",
  1708	    );
  1709	
  1710	    // M3: an import the compiler never sees.
  1711	    let source = |text: &str| source_violations("planted.rs", text).0;
  1712	    assert_eq!(
  1713	        source("#[cfg(any())]\nuse openai::Client;\n").len(),
  1714	        1,
  1715	        "M3"
  1716	    );
  1717	
  1718	    // Source variants: layout, raw identifiers, absolute paths, nested modules.
  1719	    for text in [
  1720	        "#[cfg(any())]\nuse\n    openai::Client;\n",
  1721	        "#[cfg(any())]\nuse{openai::Client};\n",
  1722	        "fn f() {} #[cfg(any())] use openai::Client;\n",
  1723	        "use {std::fmt, ::openai::Client};\n",
  1724	        "use std::{\n    fmt,\n};\nuse anthropic\n    ::Messages;\n",
  1725	        "#[cfg(any())]\nuse r#openai::Client;\n",
  1726	        "extern crate rustc_driver;\n",
  1727	        "extern crate r#popquiz;\n",
  1728	        "mod popquiz;\nextern crate popquiz;\n",
  1729	        "mod pipeline;\n#[cfg(any())]\nuse ::pipeline::Client;\n",
  1730	        "mod inner {\n    mod openai;\n}\n#[cfg(any())]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
  1731	        "fn f() {\n    use generator::Gen;\n}\n",
  1732	        // A `mod` in a block is not seen outside it.
  1733	        "fn f() { mod openai {} }\n#[cfg(any())]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
  1734	        "fn f() {\n    { mod openai {} }\n    use openai::Client;\n}\n",
  1735	        "const _: () = { mod openai {} };\n#[cfg(any())]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
  1736	        // Nor is a module's `mod` seen inside an inline module, without `super::`.
  1737	        "mod openai;\nmod inner {\n    use openai::Client;\n}\n",
  1738	        // A conditional `mod` may be absent exactly when the `use` is compiled.
  1739	        "#[cfg(not(feature = \"llm\"))]\nmod openai {}\n#[cfg(feature = \"llm\")]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
  1740	        "#[cfg(not(feature = \"llm\"))]\npub(crate) mod openai;\nuse openai::Client;\n",
  1741	        "#[allow(dead_code)]\n#[cfg_attr(any(), cfg(any()))]\nmod openai;\nuse openai::Client;\n",
  1742	        "#[path = \"elsewhere.rs\"]\nmod openai;\nuse openai::Client;\n",
  1743	        "mod openai {\n    #![cfg(not(feature = \"llm\"))]\n}\n#[cfg(feature = \"llm\")]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
  1744	        // The same gates spelled as raw identifiers.
  1745	        "#[r#cfg(not(feature = \"llm\"))]\nmod openai {}\n#[cfg(feature = \"llm\")]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
  1746	        "mod openai {\n    #![r#cfg(not(feature = \"llm\"))]\n}\n#[cfg(feature = \"llm\")]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
  1747	        "#[r#cfg_attr(any(), r#cfg(any()))]\nmod openai;\nuse openai::Client;\n",
  1748	        "#[r#path = \"elsewhere.rs\"]\nmod openai;\nuse openai::Client;\n",
  1749	    ] {
  1750	        assert_eq!(source(text).len(), 1, "{text}");
  1751	    }
  1752	
  1753	    // The room's own `mod pipeline` is local where it is seen, and only there.
  1754	    for text in [
  1755	        "mod pipeline;\npub(crate) use pipeline::admin_routes;\n",
  1756	        "mod pipeline;\nfn f() {\n    use pipeline::admin_routes;\n}\n",
  1757	        "fn f() {\n    mod pipeline {}\n    use pipeline::admin_routes;\n}\n",
  1758	        "fn f() {\n    mod pipeline {}\n    {\n        use pipeline::admin_routes;\n    }\n}\n",
  1759	        // Attributes that cannot remove it, and a gate on the item before it.
  1760	        "#[allow(dead_code)]\npub(crate) mod pipeline;\nuse pipeline::admin_routes;\n",
  1761	        "#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {}\nmod pipeline;\nuse pipeline::admin_routes;\n",
  1762	        "#[cfg(test)]\nconst A: u8 = 1;\nmod pipeline;\nuse pipeline::admin_routes;\n",
  1763	        "#![cfg_attr(docsrs, feature(doc_cfg))]\nmod pipeline;\nuse pipeline::admin_routes;\n",
  1764	    ] {
  1765	        assert!(source(text).is_empty(), "{text}");
  1766	    }
  1767	    assert_eq!(source("pub(crate) use pipeline::admin_routes;\n").len(), 1);
  1768	
  1769	    // A `mod name;` whose file gates itself, as `name.rs` or `name/mod.rs`.
  1770	    let scan = |files: &[(&str, &str)]| {
  1771	        let files: Vec<(PathBuf, String)> = files
  1772	            .iter()
  1773	            .map(|(path, text)| (PathBuf::from(path), text.to_string()))
  1774	            .collect();
  1775	        scan_files(&files, &crate_roots(&parse_toml(&manifest))).violations
  1776	    };
  1777	    let routes = "mod pipeline;\nuse pipeline::admin_routes;\n";
  1778	    for module in ["src/pipeline.rs", "src/pipeline/mod.rs"] {
  1779	        assert_eq!(
  1780	            scan(&[
  1781	                ("src/lib.rs", routes),
  1782	                (module, "#![cfg(not(feature = \"llm\"))]\n")
  1783	            ])
  1784	            .len(),
  1785	            1,
  1786	            "{module}"
  1787	        );
  1788	        assert!(
  1789	            scan(&[("src/lib.rs", routes), (module, "#![allow(dead_code)]\n")]).is_empty(),
  1790	            "{module}"
  1791	        );
  1792	    }
  1793	    // The file is the one that `mod` loads, wherever it is declared: beside a
  1794	    // `mod.rs`, in the folder named for any other file, inside inline modules.
  1795	    let gate = "#![r#cfg(not(feature = \"llm\"))]\n";
  1796	    for files in [
  1797	        [
  1798	            ("src/server/routes/mod.rs", routes),
  1799	            ("src/server/routes/pipeline.rs", gate),
  1800	        ],
  1801	        [
  1802	            ("src/server/routes.rs", routes),
  1803	            ("src/server/routes/pipeline/mod.rs", gate),
  1804	        ],
  1805	        [
  1806	            (
  1807	                "src/lib.rs",
  1808	                "mod inner {\n    mod pipeline;\n    use pipeline::admin_routes;\n}\n",
  1809	            ),
  1810	            ("src/inner/pipeline.rs", gate),
  1811	        ],
  1812	    ] {
  1813	        assert_eq!(scan(&files).len(), 1, "{files:?}");
  1814	    }
  1815	    // An inline module that may move under `#[path]` leaves its file unplaced.
  1816	    assert_eq!(
  1817	        scan(&[(
  1818	            "src/lib.rs",
  1819	            "#[path = \"elsewhere\"]\nmod inner {\n    mod pipeline;\n    use pipeline::admin_routes;\n}\n"
  1820	        )])
  1821	        .len(),
  1822	        1
  1823	    );
  1824	    // A gated file of the same name elsewhere is another module.
  1825	    for elsewhere in [
  1826	        "src/unrelated/pipeline.rs",
  1827	        "src/server/pipeline.rs",
  1828	        "src/routes/pipeline/mod.rs",
  1829	    ] {
  1830	        assert!(
  1831	            scan(&[("src/server/routes/mod.rs", routes), (elsewhere, gate)]).is_empty(),
  1832	            "{elsewhere}"
  1833	        );
  1834	    }
  1835	    // A crate root loads from its own folder, not one named for it: the
  1836	    // library, a binary the manifest names, an automatic binary.
  1837	    for (root, loaded, beside) in [
  1838	        (
  1839	            "src/lib.rs",
  1840	            "src/pipeline/mod.rs",
  1841	            "src/lib/pipeline/mod.rs",
  1842	        ),
  1843	        (
  1844	            "src/cli/smoke.rs",
  1845	            "src/cli/pipeline.rs",
  1846	            "src/cli/smoke/pipeline.rs",
  1847	        ),
  1848	        (
  1849	            "src/server/main.rs",
  1850	            "src/server/pipeline.rs",
  1851	            "src/server/main/pipeline.rs",
  1852	        ),
  1853	        (
  1854	            "src/bin/tool.rs",
  1855	            "src/bin/pipeline.rs",
  1856	            "src/bin/tool/pipeline.rs",
  1857	        ),
  1858	    ] {
  1859	        assert_eq!(scan(&[(root, routes), (loaded, gate)]).len(), 1, "{root}");
  1860	        assert!(
  1861	            scan(&[(root, routes), (loaded, ""), (beside, gate)]).is_empty(),
  1862	            "{root}"
  1863	        );
  1864	    }
  1865	
  1866	    // A `mod` in a macro's input declares nothing.
  1867	    for text in [
  1868	        "const _: &str = stringify!(mod openai {});\n#[cfg(any())]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
  1869	        "const _: &str = stringify![mod openai;];\n#[cfg(any())]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
  1870	        "m! { mod openai {} }\n#[cfg(any())]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
  1871	        "macro_rules! m { () => { mod openai {} } }\n#[cfg(any())]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
  1872	    ] {
  1873	        assert_eq!(source(text).len(), 1, "{text}");
  1874	    }
  1875	    // An import inside one is still read.
  1876	    assert_eq!(
  1877	        source(
  1878	            "cfg_if::cfg_if! {\n    if #[cfg(any())] {\n        use openai::Client;\n    }\n}\n"
  1879	        )
  1880	        .len(),
  1881	        1
  1882	    );
  1883	}
  1884	
  1885	/// Text that only looks like a dependency or an import is not one.
  1886	#[test]
  1887	fn the_readers_do_not_mistake_text_for_code() {
  1888	    let dir = crate_dir();
  1889	    // Without a byte order mark of its own, so a fixture can add one.
  1890	    let manifest = read(&dir.join("Cargo.toml")).replacen('\u{feff}', "", 1);
  1891	    // Measured against the manifest's own result, so a real dependency planted
  1892	    // in it fails the manifest check alone, not this one too.
  1893	    let baseline = manifest_violations(&dir, &manifest);
  1894	    for (what, text) in [
  1895	        (
  1896	            "a multi-line string",
  1897	            planted(
  1898	                &manifest,
  1899	                "[package]",
  1900	                "metadata.example = \"\"\"\n[dependencies]\nopenai = \"0.1\"\n\"\"\"",
  1901	            ),
  1902	        ),
  1903	        (
  1904	            "a multi-line literal string",
  1905	            planted(
  1906	                &manifest,
  1907	                "[package]",
  1908	                "metadata.example = '''\n[dependencies]\nopenai = \"0.1\"\n'''",
  1909	            ),
  1910	        ),
  1911	        (
  1912	            "a comment",
  1913	            planted(&manifest, "[dependencies]", "# openai = \"0.1\""),
  1914	        ),
  1915	        (
  1916	            "an empty patch table",
  1917	            format!("{manifest}\n[patch.crates-io]\n"),
  1918	        ),
  1919	        ("a byte order mark", format!("\u{feff}{manifest}")),
  1920	    ] {
  1921	        assert_eq!(manifest_violations(&dir, &text), baseline, "{what}");
  1922	    }
  1923	
  1924	    for text in [
  1925	        "/* an example:\nuse openai::Client;\n*/\n",
  1926	        "/* nested /* comments */\nuse openai::Client;\n*/\n",
  1927	        "// use openai::Client;\n",
  1928	        "const EXAMPLE: &str = r#\"\nuse openai::Client;\n\"#;\n",
  1929	        "const EXAMPLE: &str = \"\\\"\nuse openai::Client;\n\";\n",
  1930	        "const QUOTE: char = '\"';\nconst EXAMPLE: &str = \"use openai::Client;\";\n",
  1931	        "fn f<'a>(x: &'a str) -> impl Sized + use<'a> { x }\n",
  1932	        "fn pipeline_name() -> &'static str { \"pipeline\" }\n",
  1933	    ] {
  1934	        assert!(source_violations("text.rs", text).0.is_empty(), "{text}");
  1935	    }
  1936	}
  1937	
  1938	/// Cargo can lock two packages of one name and version from different sources,
  1939	/// and then names the source in each dependency line.
  1940	#[test]
  1941	fn the_lock_walk_tells_sources_apart() {
  1942	    let lock = r#"
  1943	version = 4
  1944	
  1945	[[package]]
  1946	name = "room"
  1947	version = "0.1.0"
  1948	dependencies = [
  1949	 "shim 1.0.0 (git+https://example.com/shim)",
  1950	]
  1951	
  1952	[[package]]
  1953	name = "shim"
  1954	version = "1.0.0"
  1955	source = "registry+https://github.com/rust-lang/crates.io-index"
  1956	
  1957	[[package]]
  1958	name = "shim"
  1959	version = "1.0.0"
  1960	source = "git+https://example.com/shim"
  1961	dependencies = [
  1962	 "openai",
  1963	]
  1964	
  1965	[[package]]
  1966	name = "openai"
  1967	version = "0.1.0"
  1968	source = "registry+https://github.com/rust-lang/crates.io-index"
  1969	"#;
  1970	    let found = lock_violations(lock);
  1971	    assert!(
  1972	        found.iter().any(|violation| violation.contains("openai")),
  1973	        "the git `shim` reaches openai: {found:?}"
  1974	    );
  1975	    let packages = lock_packages(lock);
  1976	    let reached: Vec<_> = room_subgraph(&packages)
  1977	        .iter()
  1978	        .map(|package| (package.name.clone(), package.source.clone()))
  1979	        .collect();
  1980	    assert_eq!(
  1981	        reached.len(),
  1982	        3,
  1983	        "room, the git shim and openai: {reached:?}"
  1984	    );
  1985	}
  1986	
  1987	/// The shape `cargo generate-lockfile` writes for one crate from two git
  1988	/// repositories (one on a branch): each package's source carries the commit it
  1989	/// is pinned to, and each dependency line names the source without it.
  1990	#[test]
  1991	fn the_lock_walk_matches_git_sources_without_their_commit() {
  1992	    let lock = r#"
  1993	version = 4
  1994	
  1995	[[package]]
  1996	name = "room"
  1997	version = "0.1.0"
  1998	dependencies = [
  1999	 "shim 1.0.0 (git+https://example.com/b?branch=dev)",
  2000	 "shim 1.0.0 (git+https://example.com/a)",
  2001	]
  2002	
  2003	[[package]]
  2004	name = "shim"
  2005	version = "1.0.0"
  2006	source = "git+https://example.com/b?branch=dev#84b81c3f13100567babfe14dea141f96d2d8f1f4"
  2007	dependencies = [
  2008	 "openai",
  2009	]
  2010	
  2011	[[package]]
  2012	name = "shim"
  2013	version = "1.0.0"
  2014	source = "git+https://example.com/a#2e4a07543fbfb2fbda07356922c0fa81f18b9bbb"
  2015	
  2016	[[package]]
  2017	name = "openai"
  2018	version = "0.1.0"
  2019	source = "registry+https://github.com/rust-lang/crates.io-index"
  2020	"#;
  2021	    let packages = lock_packages(lock);
  2022	    let reached: Vec<_> = room_subgraph(&packages)
  2023	        .iter()
  2024	        .map(|package| (package.name.clone(), package.source.clone()))
  2025	        .collect();
  2026	    assert_eq!(reached.len(), 4, "room, both shims and openai: {reached:?}");
  2027	    assert!(
  2028	        lock_violations(lock)
  2029	            .iter()
  2030	            .any(|violation| violation.contains("openai")),
  2031	        "the branch `shim` reaches openai"
  2032	    );
  2033	    // The branch still tells the two repositories' sources apart.
  2034	    assert_eq!(
  2035	        resolve(
  2036	            &packages,
  2037	            "shim 1.0.0 (git+https://example.com/b?branch=dev)"
  2038	        ),
  2039	        vec![1]
  2040	    );
  2041	    assert_eq!(
  2042	        resolve(&packages, "shim 1.0.0 (git+https://example.com/a)"),
  2043	        vec![2]
  2044	    );
  2045	}
  2046	
  2047	/// The shape `cargo generate-lockfile` writes when `[replace]` redirects the
  2048	/// room's git `shim` to another repository: the replacement is no dependency
  2049	/// line, only a `replace` field on the package it stands in for.
  2050	#[test]
  2051	fn the_lock_walk_follows_replacements() {
  2052	    // The second repository's URL ends in `)`, which Cargo keeps in the source.
  2053	    for b in [
  2054	        "https://example.com/b?branch=dev",
  2055	        "https://example.com/shim-b)",
  2056	    ] {
  2057	        replacement_lock_reaches_openai(b);
  2058	    }
  2059	}
  2060	
  2061	fn replacement_lock_reaches_openai(b: &str) {
  2062	    let lock = &format!(
  2063	        r#"
  2064	version = 4
  2065	
  2066	[[package]]
  2067	name = "room"
  2068	version = "0.1.0"
  2069	dependencies = [
  2070	 "shim 1.0.0 (git+https://example.com/a)",
  2071	]
  2072	
  2073	[[package]]
  2074	name = "shim"
  2075	version = "1.0.0"
  2076	source = "git+{b}#84b81c3f13100567babfe14dea141f96d2d8f1f4"
  2077	dependencies = [
  2078	 "openai",
  2079	]
  2080	
  2081	[[package]]
  2082	name = "shim"
  2083	version = "1.0.0"
  2084	source = "git+https://example.com/a#2e4a07543fbfb2fbda07356922c0fa81f18b9bbb"
  2085	replace = "shim 1.0.0 (git+{b})"
  2086	
  2087	[[package]]
  2088	name = "openai"
  2089	version = "0.1.0"
  2090	source = "registry+https://github.com/rust-lang/crates.io-index"
  2091	"#
  2092	    );
  2093	    let packages = lock_packages(lock);
  2094	    let reached = room_subgraph(&packages).len();
  2095	    assert_eq!(reached, 4, "room, both shims and openai, via {b}");
  2096	    assert!(
  2097	        lock_violations(lock)
  2098	            .iter()
  2099	            .any(|violation| violation.contains("openai")),
  2100	        "the replacement `shim` reaches openai"
  2101	    );
  2102	}

commit f82f7e011be484758f794150815ac1b4e2d74ade
Author: Michelle Rojas <mrojas1028@gmail.com>
Commit: Michelle Rojas <mrojas1028@gmail.com>

    Read raw attributes, normalized URLs, module files and lock replacements
    
    Codex pass 4 found two bypasses and two scanning defects:
    
    - F: `#[r#cfg(...)]` is `#[cfg(...)]`; the gate check now matches raw
      identifiers, outer and inner.
    - G: a [replace] key's URL is read the way Cargo's URL parser reads it:
      `\` separates path segments, `.`/`..` (and `%2e`) segments resolve, and
      tabs and newlines are dropped, before the name is taken.
    - H: a self-gated module file makes only the `mod name;` that loads it
      conditional, found by the file it resolves to (beside a mod.rs or crate
      root, in the folder named for any other file, inside inline modules),
      not every module with that basename.
    - I: the lock walk follows the `replace = "..."` field Cargo writes on a
      replaced package, so the replacement and what it pulls in are checked.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

commit e90be8bb332b730ccb1278689432d6cd6f746afd
Author: Michelle Rojas <mrojas1028@gmail.com>
Commit: Michelle Rojas <mrojas1028@gmail.com>

    Count only unconditional modules, and read every replace key form
    
    The third review found two more ways past the check. A `mod openai` under one
    `cfg` exempted a `use openai::…` under the opposite `cfg`, so the import
    reached the real crate whenever it was compiled; and a `[replace]` key written
    as `openai@0.1.0` or with a source URL was read as some other name.
    
    A module now makes an import local only when nothing can remove it: no outer
    `cfg`, `cfg_attr` or `path` attribute, no inner `#![cfg…]` in its body, and,
    for `mod name;`, no self-gating `name.rs` or `name/mod.rs`. Replace keys are
    read as Cargo's package ID specs.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

commit f533c91b21fc085fd851a3d1fd4b599c7dc4b481
Author: Michelle Rojas <mrojas1028@gmail.com>
Commit: Michelle Rojas <mrojas1028@gmail.com>

    Keep block-local modules, workspace specs and git pins in view
    
    The second review found three gaps in the dependency check. A `mod` declared
    inside a function exempted a matching import outside it; dependencies
    inherited from `[workspace.dependencies]` were never read; and a lock with a
    git dependency made the walk panic, since Cargo pins a package's git source to
    a commit that its dependency lines leave out.
    
    Scopes now follow braces and stop at module boundaries, the workspace table is
    read like any other (and inheriting from a workspace above the room is
    refused), and git sources match without their commit but keep their branch.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

commit 92ae9e5bc84eca5b491b55807935eb5a4f4941c9
Author: Michelle Rojas <mrojas1028@gmail.com>
Commit: Michelle Rojas <mrojas1028@gmail.com>

    Parse the manifest, lock and source instead of matching lines
    
    The first review found layouts that hid a dependency from the line
    matchers: dotted and quoted keys, escaped or multi-line strings, quoted
    or spaced headers, imports split across lines or written `use{…}`, raw
    identifiers, and `::name` beside a local module of that name. It also
    found false alarms from comments, strings and an empty patch table.
    
    The manifest and lock now go through a small TOML parser and the source
    through a small Rust tokenizer, both in the test file. A module only
    excuses a `use` in the module that declares it, never for `::name` or
    `extern crate`. The lock walk tells packages apart by source. Each
    variant the review reproduced is a fixture.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

commit f097a78c5c198b18c54079fd6e8c47cb603a7093
Author: Michelle Rojas <mrojas1028@gmail.com>
Commit: Michelle Rojas <mrojas1028@gmail.com>

    Test that the room depends on no generator, verifier or LLM code
    
    AC-1 held by convention only: nothing in `just test` looked at the room's
    dependencies. popquiz/tests/dependencies.rs reads the manifest, the lock
    and the source as text and refuses a denied crate name (anthropic, openai,
    llm, generator, miri, pipeline, popquiz, rustc_*) in any dependency table,
    anywhere in the room's lock subgraph, or as the root of a `use` or
    `extern crate`, and refuses a `path` dependency into pipeline/.
    
    It matches crate names, never folders, so the room's own folder name
    (popquiz/, also the Python package's name) cannot trip it. The planted
    mutations stay in the file as fixtures.
    
    Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

-- rule line candidates --
   124	
   125	- **Offline question pipeline** — generate → pinned rustc + Miri verify →
   126	  dedupe → organizer review → a growing bank (public; see `bank/README.md`). No deadline.
   127	- **Live room app** — join by code, read code, answer, reveal, aggregate.
   128	
   129	Plus **`mvp/`**: the hand-run HTML projector deck from before the room existed.
   130	**Retired 2026-10-11.** The room and `docs/RUNBOOK.md` run the segment, and the
   131	backup on the night is the static `<id>.html` that `just schedule` writes, not
   132	the deck. Don't build decks. `mvp/` stays for its templates
   133	(`PRACTICE-RUN.md`, `FIELD-NOTES-TEMPLATE.md`), *The shape* in its README, and
   134	the `answer-history.json` record.
   135	
   136	## House rules
   137	
   138	- **Never write down what a program prints.** Run it. Correct answers come from
   139	  `verified.json`, which is written by the verifier, never by hand (AC-7).
   140	- **The segment is one question, 3–5 minutes, scheduled last** (D-7, Story B10 /
   141	  AC-89…AC-92). Not a round. Anything that assumes several questions a night is
   142	  stale.
   143	- **Answer position is drawn uniformly from the date and nothing else**
   192	  that one test.
   193	
   194	## Conventions
   195	
   196	- **`lib.rs`, `main.rs` and `mod.rs` are required, not banned** (ADR-0005,
   197	  "File names"). The crate root is `lib.rs`, the server binary is
   198	  `server/main.rs`, and every directory module is `<folder>/mod.rs`, never a
   199	  `<folder>.rs` beside its folder. Every other file is a noun named for what
   200	  it holds.
   201	- **Room commands take `--room <url>`.** `just schedule` and `just sync` need
   202	  it (`--no-push` replaces it on `schedule` when the room is down). `just
   203	  --list` shows these recipes only as `*ARGS`, so read the required arguments
   204	  in `docs/RUNBOOK.md` before running one, rather than learning them from a
   205	  failed run.

unrouted and were kept out of both rounds.

### Touchpoint T-23 reopened — the receipt shows steps, 2026-09-19

After the amendments passed the judge (round 2, 8/10), the client read the legacy
receipt and said it should be short phrases, not sentences. Asked what the room
is verifying and what *computer* means, the client concluded that *Verified* is
a claim and a room should see the verification process instead. Applied as D-25:
the wall receipt is a step list headed *How we know*. Four lines for a record that
ran (a `legacy` record included), three for a does-not-compile record. Every line
renders only if the record holds its step. The machine detail and AC-71's *The
machine checked the answer only. An organizer approved the explanation.* are on
take-it-home. Edited: `SPEC.md` §3.2, G-7, §7.5, §11, §13; `EVALUATION.md` AC-43,
AC-71, AC-87; `USER_STORIES.md` AC-71, AC-87 and a new amendments subsection;
`BUILDPLAN.md` T-05, T-07, T-12 and the minimum viable cut; `DESIGN.md`; and the
two prototypes.

**One case the client did not see:** when undefined behavior is the declared
answer (AC-9), Miri does not run clean, so that line reads *✓ Miri flagged
undefined behavior*. **Correction:** the client was told the take-it-home line
would say the explanation is *human-written*. That is false, since the generator
drafts it, so take-it-home carries the already-approved closing pair instead.

**D-25 judge, one round: 6/10, FAIL on one, applied.** The blocking finding was mine:
AC-43 had a fixture for a record with no Miri result, which §3.2 does not allow
(it is neither complete, `legacy` nor does-not-compile, so it renders no receipt).
Dropped. Also applied: the machine provenance marker on the *How we know*
heading, which AC-74 needs and the rewrite had lost; the receipt no longer says
*the verifier took* the steps, since a `legacy` record's Miri step ran outside it;
AC-87 tests take-it-home's Miri row for a `legacy` record; the no-sentence test is
now terminal punctuation plus a closed word list; T-07's stale *receipt's
provenance line* is the beats' human marker; *does-not-compile* is spelled one way.
The judge's AC-40 citation was removed: a ✓ marks a step done, not a pass.
Not re-judged after these fixes.

**D-25 judge round 2: 8/10, PASS, no blocking finding.** Applied: the Miri line
gates on `miri` present with `output_matched` and only its wording follows
`clean`, so a UB-answer question still gets four lines; *Compiled* gates on `runs`
present, so a `panic` answer with empty `stdout` still gets four, with a fixture
added to AC-43; *the list above* is named *the four-line list*; AC-43's
no-punctuation rule is scoped to the wall receipt, since take-it-home carries
AC-71's sentence; the minimum viable cut says a legacy question's Miri caveat
has no surface until T-12 ships. **Not applied:** the judge said the wall's machine
marker was unstyled. `.provenance.machine` is defined in
`prototypes/_shared/tokens.css:282`, and the heading text differs from the
human marker's. Stopped at a pass in round 2 of the three allowed.

            "src/bin/tool.rs",

    // A `mod` in a macro's input declares nothing.
    for text in [
        "const _: &str = stringify!(mod openai {});\n#[cfg(any())]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
        "const _: &str = stringify![mod openai;];\n#[cfg(any())]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
        "m! { mod openai {} }\n#[cfg(any())]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
        "macro_rules! m { () => { mod openai {} } }\n#[cfg(any())]\nuse openai::Client;\n",
    ] {
        assert_eq!(source(text).len(), 1, "{text}");
    }
    // An import inside one is still read.
    assert_eq!(
        source(
            "cfg_if::cfg_if! {\n    if #[cfg(any())] {\n        use openai::Client;\n    }\n}\n"
        )
        .len(),
        1
    );
}

/// Text that only looks like a dependency or an import is not one.
#[test]
fn the_readers_do_not_mistake_text_for_code() {
    let dir = crate_dir();
    // Without a byte order mark of its own, so a fixture can add one.
    let manifest = read(&dir.join("Cargo.toml")).replacen('\u{feff}', "", 1);
    // Measured against the manifest's own result, so a real dependency planted
    // in it fails the manifest check alone, not this one too.
    let baseline = manifest_violations(&dir, &manifest);
    for (what, text) in [
        (
            "a multi-line string",
            planted(
                &manifest,
                "[package]",
                "metadata.example = \"\"\"\n[dependencies]\nopenai = \"0.1\"\n\"\"\"",
            ),
        ),
        (
            "a multi-line literal string",
            planted(
                &manifest,
                "[package]",
                "metadata.example = '''\n[dependencies]\nopenai = \"0.1\"\n'''",
            ),
        ),
        (
            "a comment",
            planted(&manifest, "[dependencies]", "# openai = \"0.1\""),
        ),
        (
            "an empty patch table",
            format!("{manifest}\n[patch.crates-io]\n"),
        ),
        ("a byte order mark", format!("\u{feff}{manifest}")),
    ] {
        assert_eq!(manifest_violations(&dir, &text), baseline, "{what}");
    }

    for text in [
        "/* an example:\nuse openai::Client;\n*/\n",

[parallel]
test: test-room test-pipeline test-web test-organizer

# Checks formatting (popquiz/rustfmt.toml), then builds and tests the room crate with whatever cargo this machine has.
test-room:
    cd popquiz && cargo fmt --check
    cd popquiz && cargo test --offline --locked

# Organizer UI stays checked against the room API it imports.
test-organizer:
    cd prototypes/organizer-tui && cargo test --offline --locked
    cd prototypes/organizer-tui && cargo clippy --offline --locked --all-targets -- -D warnings
    cd prototypes/organizer-tui && cargo fmt --check

    python3 prototypes/organizer-tui/tests/terminal_repeat.py
    python3 -m unittest discover -s prototypes/organizer-tui/tests -p 'test_terminal_repeat.py'

# The pipeline's decision logic, against StubRunner's recorded outputs.
#
# tests/sandbox is ignored here and nowhere else: it is the AC-12 containment suite
# and it needs a real container, which this hook is forbidden from having. Ignoring
# the directory means its conftest is never even imported — and that conftest
# refuses to run unasked, so the suite has no way to look green without a container.
test-pipeline:
    cd pipeline && uv run --offline --no-sync pytest --ignore=tests/sandbox

# Node's built-in runner. No package.json, no node_modules, nothing to install.
# The glob is quoted so node expands it, not the shell: with no package.json
# anywhere, node resolves a bare directory argument as a module specifier and
# fails looking for an index, rather than scanning it for tests.
test-web:
    node --test 'web/test/*.test.js'

# `a11y` is not a dependency: `test` already runs web/test/a11y.test.js (the
# test-web glob); `just a11y` is the same suite with its matrix printed.
#
# Everything that exists today, then an honest list of what does not.
# Green by contract: the pending suites are named here, never invoked here.

FAIL: against the pinned 1fbc6bd...1b42737 comparison, the original J/K/L/M repros are fixed, but four compiler/Cargo-accepted variants still bypass the source check, including three regressions from the new module-resolution logic. HEAD’s 10 dependency tests pass, M1–M3 are independently rejected, and the Rust checks passed 360 tests with 4 ignored; full `just test` completion was blocked by sandbox-denied uv-cache writes affecting the pipeline suite and one Python-backed web test, not by patch failures. The diff adds only the test file, with no production changes or folder-based denylist matching (`popquiz` denotes the Python package), and no worktree source or configuration files were edited during this review.

Full review comments:

- [P2] [Major] Bound macro-input imports to their token tree — /Users/michellerojas/rust-nyc-pop-quiz-worktrees/pq-47-room-dep-denylist/popquiz/tests/dependencies.rs:1155-1158
  Appending `macro_rules! m { (use std) => {} } #[cfg(any())] use openai::Client;` to `src/copy.rs` compiles, but all 10 dependency tests still pass. The reader treats the matcher’s `use std` as an import and searches beyond the macro’s closing delimiter for a semicolon; it consumes the real `use openai::Client;` without inspecting its root. J’s macro-input marking prevents false module declarations but does not prevent this remaining M3 bypass. Bound import consumption to its enclosing token tree while retaining detection of actual imports inside macro input.

- [P2] [Major] Respect disabled automatic binary discovery — /Users/michellerojas/rust-nyc-pop-quiz-worktrees/pq-47-room-dep-denylist/popquiz/tests/dependencies.rs:1302-1307
  The real manifest already sets `autobins = false`, but this function nevertheless classifies every `src/bin/*.rs` file as a crate root. A compiler-accepted repro is `mod bin;` in `src/lib.rs`, `mod helper;` in `src/bin/mod.rs`, `mod openai; #[cfg(any())] use openai::Client;` in `src/bin/helper.rs`, and `#![cfg(any())]` in `src/bin/helper/openai/mod.rs`. Rust loads the gated child under `helper/`, whereas the scanner incorrectly looks directly under `src/bin/` and exempts the import as local. HEAD passes all 10 tests on this copy; f82f7e0 rejects it. Only apply automatic-root classification when Cargo’s discovery settings enable it.

- [P2] [Major] Preserve the loading context of path-attributed modules — /Users/michellerojas/rust-nyc-pop-quiz-worktrees/pq-47-room-dep-denylist/popquiz/tests/dependencies.rs:1335-1338
  For `#[path = "support/helper.rs"] mod helper;` in `src/lib.rs`, Rust resolves a `mod openai;` inside that file to `src/support/openai/mod.rs`, not `src/support/helper/openai/mod.rs`. Put `mod openai; #[cfg(any())] use openai::Client;` in `helper.rs` and `#![cfg(any())]` in the actual child: this compiles, and HEAD passes all 10 dependency tests, while f82f7e0 rejects the import. The single-base change therefore regresses H for modules loaded through `#[path]`; marking the parent declaration conditional does not propagate its loading context into the separately scanned file. Track that context, or conservatively treat the child location as unresolved, rather than infer its base solely from the filename.

- [P2] [Major] Normalize parent components in manifest target paths — /Users/michellerojas/rust-nyc-pop-quiz-worktrees/pq-47-room-dep-denylist/popquiz/tests/dependencies.rs:1293-1297
  Adding a binary named `review-entry` with `path = "src/server/../entry.rs"`, placing `mod openai; #[cfg(any())] use openai::Client; fn main() {}` in `src/entry.rs`, and placing `#![cfg(any())]` in `src/openai/mod.rs` produces another bypass: Cargo accepts and normalizes the target path, rustc compiles it, and HEAD passes all 10 tests. `clean()` removes `.` but retains `..`, so the root set does not match the scanner’s `src/entry.rs`; module resolution then incorrectly checks under `src/entry/`. Changing only the manifest spelling to `src/entry.rs` makes the source test fail, and f82f7e0 also rejects the original repro. Normalize parent components consistently with Cargo before comparing target paths.
